Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi. Um, well, I guess the main thing is I just get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. Like, I’ll be sitting there trying to work on a job application—which is a total nightmare anyway, because the websites they use now are so annoying. My buddy tried to apply to this warehouse job last week and it made him retype his whole resume after he already uploaded the file, which is just stupid. 

But yeah, I'll be trying to do the application, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone lights up, or I just get some random idea in my head, and suddenly I'm off doing something completely different before I even notice. Like yesterday, I heard this really loud muffler outside, and it reminded me of my cousin's old Honda. We used to drive that thing out to the lake back in high school and the radio only played cassettes... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just end up somewhere else entirely and I can't stay on track.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, constantly. Like, if I'm sitting down with someone, my leg is almost always bouncing up and down. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just digging in my pocket messing with my keys, and I don't even realize I'm doing it until someone points it out. My ex used to hate the pen clicking thing. She’d actually reach over and snatch it out of my hand if we were trying to watch a movie. We used to watch a lot of those true crime documentaries, you know? The ones where they stretch it out into like ten episodes even though they could have told the whole story in two hours. I can't stand when they do that, just dragging out the same interviews over and over... but yeah, I definitely can't just sit still. I'm always moving something.

interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I cut people off a lot, especially my mom or the counselor, because an answer pops into my head and I say it before they’re done talking.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Even in elementary school, teachers were always moving my seat because I talked out of turn, forgot homework, and rushed through worksheets with careless mistakes.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I guess the main thing is I just get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. My buddy tried to apply to this warehouse job last week and it made him retype his whole resume after he already uploaded the file, which is just stupid. \n\nBut yeah, I'll be trying to do the application, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone lights up, or I just get some random idea in my head, and suddenly I'm off doing something completely different before I even notice. Like yesterday, I heard this really loud muffler outside, and it reminded me of my cousin's old Honda. We used to drive that thing out to the lake back in high school and the radio only played cassettes... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just end up somewhere else entirely and I can't stay on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, constantly. Like, if I'm sitting down with someone, my leg is almost always bouncing up and down. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just digging in my pocket messing with my keys, and I don't even realize I'm doing it until someone points it out. My ex used to hate the pen clicking thing. She’d actually reach over and snatch it out of my hand if we were trying to watch a movie. We used to watch a lot of those true crime documentaries, you know? The ones where they stretch it out into like ten episodes even though they could have told the whole story in two hours. I can't stand when they do that, just dragging out the same interviews over and over... but yeah, I definitely can't just sit still. I'm always moving something.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I do things without thinking. I actually think about stuff a lot before I do it. Like if I'm going to buy something, I'll usually hold off and think on it. \n\nExcept, man, everything is just so expensive right now anyway, so you kind of have to wait. I went to get a burger yesterday and a basic combo meal was like fifteen bucks. Can you believe that? For a drive-thru! It used to be you could get a whole meal for five dollars. I remember back in high school me and my friends would go to Wendy's after we got out and we'd get so much food with just the crumpled up cash we had in our pockets. We used to hang out in that parking lot for hours just talking about whatever. The manager hated us being there, but he never actually kicked us out... \n\nBut yeah, what were we saying? Impulsive? No, I don't really just jump into things like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you have trouble controlling your emotions?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Um, I don't think so. I mean, I get mad sometimes, sure, but I don't think I fly off the handle or anything. I don't really yell at people. Usually, if I'm upset, I just kind of keep it to myself or try to distract myself. 

Like the other day, I was trying to fix this leaky pipe under my sink, and I just could not get the wrench to grip the stupid bolt. I was getting so frustrated I actually threw the wrench across the kitchen. It dented the drywall right next to the fridge, which sucks because I'm renting and my landlord is already super strict about the security deposit. He made a huge deal about this tiny scratch on the floor when I moved in, took like five pictures of it on his iPad. He’s one of those guys who acts like he’s running a luxury hotel when it’s just a crappy one-bedroom apartment with paper-thin walls. I can hear the guy upstairs walking around all night, it sounds like he’s wearing bowling shoes... 

But, uh, anyway, no, I wouldn't say my emotions are out of control. Just normal stuff, I guess.

Yeah, it definitely causes problems. It's actually really frustrating because I want to get my life going, you know? I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work, I'm great with cars. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I mess up the paperwork and have to redo the applications. Which sucks because I really want to get into a good shop. My uncle used to run a garage out by the old highway, and I spent practically my whole summer there when I was fourteen. He had this classic 1969 Mustang Mach 1 he was restoring. It was originally that bright grabber blue, but the guy who owned it before him tried to paint it black himself in his driveway, and it looked terrible. My uncle spent weeks just sanding that thing down. He used to let me sort the spark plugs and clean the tools. He always said you could tell a good mechanic by how clean they kept their wrenches. He had this whole pegboard system that was perfectly outlined with a sharpie so you knew exactly where everything went. I should probably do that in my own apartment, honestly, maybe I wouldn't lose my keys every single morning... 

But yeah, I’m just stuck right now. It definitely gets in the way.

Yeah, school was pretty rough. Following instructions was always hard because I just couldn't keep my focus on what the teacher was saying. Even now, like, I can start watching a repair video or reading the manual for a new tool, but after a few minutes I realize I’ve just been staring at it and I have to back it up because none of it stuck. 

It's like when I was trying to figure out how to use this new OBD2 scanner I bought. It was supposed to be Bluetooth, right? You hook it up to your phone so you can read the engine codes without having to use the clunky handheld ones. I got it on Amazon, one of those lightning deals they always have. I swear, I spend way too much time looking at those deals. Last week I almost bought a set of mixing bowls just because they were 40% off, and I don't even bake. I don't even know where I'd put them, my kitchen cabinets are already full of random junk I never use. I have a panini press I used exactly once, like three years ago, and now it just takes up an entire shelf... 

Wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, school. Yeah, I just drifted off a lot. Still do, I guess.

Yeah, I was definitely one of those kids who couldn't sit still. I played a ton of soccer back then, which was great because it burned off all that extra energy. I was on this travel team for a few years, we actually went all the way to a tournament in Florida once. It was August, though, so it was ridiculously hot. I think... well, it's probably the getting distracted part. The main thing is I get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. Like, I’ll be trying to work on an application for a shop, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone buzzes, or I just get some random idea, and suddenly I'm doing something completely different before I even notice.

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think so, yeah. Even in elementary school, it was always a thing. I remember my teachers were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever was next to me. I'd always forget my homework, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets and make all these stupid, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done with it. 

I remember one time in third grade, Mrs. Gable—she was this older lady who always smelled like peppermint and hairspray—she moved my desk right next to hers. She had this massive jar of peppermints on her desk, the kind with the red and white stripes. I actually hate peppermint. It reminds me of toothpaste. I don't get why people like mint chocolate, either. It's like brushing your teeth and eating a candy bar at the same time. My mom used to buy mint chocolate chip ice cream all the time because it was her favorite, and I’d always have to eat around the little chocolate flakes because the mint part was just gross. She’d get the big gallon tubs from Costco... man, Costco is dangerous. You go in for paper towels and come out with a kayak and a 50-pound bag of rice. 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, being a little kid. Yeah, it’s always been like that, as far back as I can remember.

Well, you'd think so, right? Like, if I'm really into something, I should be able to just lock in. But honestly, even with stuff I really care about, it's a struggle. Like, I love fixing things, right? So I'll start watching a repair video or I'll pull out the manual for a new tool. But after just a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and none of it actually stuck. I have to keep backing the video up or re-reading the same paragraph over and over. 

It's so frustrating, especially with those repair videos. Some of those guys on YouTube are great, but others just talk way too much before they even get to the point. Like, there's this one channel I watch sometimes, and the guy always spends the first ten minutes talking about the history of the engine block or something. I mean, it's cool if you're a history buff, but I just want to know how to set the timing belt. My dad used to be like that, actually. You'd ask him how to fix a leaky faucet, and he'd start explaining the municipal water system. He worked for the city water department for like thirty years. He always had these crazy stories about the stuff they'd find in the sewers. Once they found a whole... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, focusing on stuff I like. Yeah, even then, my brain just kind of checks out after a few minutes.

Usually, if I'm upset, I just kind of keep it to myself or try to distract myself. \n\nLike the other day, I was trying to fix this leaky pipe under my sink, and I just could not get the wrench to grip the stupid bolt. I was getting so frustrated I actually threw the wrench across the kitchen. It dented the drywall right next to the fridge, which sucks because I'm renting and my landlord is already super strict about the security deposit. He made a huge deal about this tiny scratch on the floor when I moved in, took like five pictures of it on his iPad. He’s one of those guys who acts like he’s running a luxury hotel when it’s just a crappy one-bedroom apartment with paper-thin walls. I can hear the guy upstairs walking around all night, it sounds like he’s wearing bowling shoes... \n\nBut, uh, anyway, no, I wouldn't say my emotions are out of control. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these problems with staying on track and feeling restless?\n\nPatient: It's been forever, honestly. Like, even back in elementary school, I remember my teachers were always moving my desk around. They'd put me right next to their desk, or in the back corner, because I was always talking out of turn or forgetting to bring my homework in. And whenever we had worksheets, I'd just blast through them and make all these stupid careless mistakes. \n\nSpeaking of elementary school, they just tore down my old school last month. It's so weird driving by there now and it's just this massive pile of dirt and bricks. They're supposed to be building some kind of mixed-use development, you know, with the overpriced apartments on top and the empty retail spaces on the bottom? Every town is building those now. It just looks so generic. But anyway, yeah, I've always been like this. It's not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless are causing problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. It's actually really frustrating because I want to get my life going, you know? I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work, I'm great with cars. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I mess up the paperwork and have to redo the applications. So I'm just kind of stuck and not moving forward. \n\nWhich sucks because I really want to get into a good shop. My uncle used to run a garage out by the old highway, and I spent practically my whole summer there when I was fourteen. He had this classic 1969 Mustang Mach 1 he was restoring. It was originally that bright grabber blue, but the guy who owned it before him tried to paint it black himself in his driveway, and it looked terrible. My uncle spent weeks just sanding that thing down. He used to let me sort the spark plugs and clean the tools. He always said you could tell a good mechanic by how clean they kept their wrenches. He had this whole pegboard system that was perfectly outlined with a sharpie so you knew exactly where everything went. I should probably do that in my own apartment, honestly, maybe I wouldn't lose my keys every single morning... \n\nBut yeah, I’m just stuck right now. It definitely gets in the way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or following instructions in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, school was pretty rough. Following instructions was always hard because I just couldn't keep my focus on what the teacher was saying. It was supposed to be Bluetooth, right? You hook it up to your phone so you can read the engine codes without having to use the clunky handheld ones. Last week I almost bought a set of mixing bowls just because they were 40% off, and I don't even bake. I played a ton of soccer back then, which was great because it burned off all that extra energy. Like, the kind of heat where the air feels heavy and you're sweating before you even get out of the car. I don't know how they do it. \n\nBut yeah, I don't play soccer anymore, and it's been driving me crazy. Sitting around the house now, I just get this feeling like I'm crawling out of my skin. I'll pick it up just to check the time, and then I see a notification from YouTube about a video on how to rebuild a carburetor. Next thing I know, I've spent two hours watching videos about vintage motorcycles. I don't even own a motorcycle. I've always wanted one, though. My buddy Dave has this sick Triumph Bonneville, it's matte black with these custom leather saddlebags. He let me ride it once in an empty parking lot, and the torque on that thing is insane. Yeah, I do that a lot. It's really bad with my mom, or like, when I was talking to the career counselor last week. They'll be in the middle of asking me something, and the answer just pops into my head, and I just blurt it out before they even finish their sentence. It's like I can't hold it in.

He used to be a trucker, drove big rigs all over the country. He actually brought me a real cowboy hat from Texas once when I was a kid. It was way too big for me, made me look like a mushroom, but I wore it everywhere. I even tried to wear it to bed once, but my mom made me take it off because she said it was dirty. She was always weird about germs. Like, she wouldn't even let me eat food if it fell on the floor for like half a second. The five-second rule didn't exist in our house. 

Anyway... yeah, I interrupt people a lot. I don't mean to be rude, it just comes out.

We used to drive that thing out to the lake back in high school and the radio only played cassettes... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just end up somewhere else entirely and I can't stay on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, constantly. Like, if I'm sitting down with someone, my leg is almost always bouncing up and down. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just digging in my pocket messing with my keys, and I don't even realize I'm doing it until someone points it out. My ex used to hate the pen clicking thing. She’d actually reach over and snatch it out of my hand if we were trying to watch a movie. We used to watch a lot of those true crime documentaries, you know? The ones where they stretch it out into like ten episodes even though they could have told the whole story in two hours. I can't stand when they do that, just dragging out the same interviews over and over... but yeah, I definitely can't just sit still. I'm always moving something.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I do things without thinking. I actually think about stuff a lot before I do it. Like if I'm going to buy something, I'll usually hold off and think on it. \n\nExcept, man, everything is just so expensive right now anyway, so you kind of have to wait. I went to get a burger yesterday and a basic combo meal was like fifteen bucks. Can you believe that? For a drive-thru! It used to be you could get a whole meal for five dollars. I remember back in high school me and my friends would go to Wendy's after we got out and we'd get so much food with just the crumpled up cash we had in our pockets. We used to hang out in that parking lot for hours just talking about whatever. The manager hated us being there, but he never actually kicked us out... \n\nBut yeah, what were we saying? Impulsive? No, I don't really just jump into things like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble controlling your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think so. I mean, I get mad sometimes, sure, but I don't think I fly off the handle or anything. I don't really yell at people. Usually, if I'm upset, I just kind of keep it to myself or try to distract myself. \n\nLike the other day, I was trying to fix this leaky pipe under my sink, and I just could not get the wrench to grip the stupid bolt. I was getting so frustrated I actually threw the wrench across the kitchen. It dented the drywall right next to the fridge, which sucks because I'm renting and my landlord is already super strict about the security deposit. He made a huge deal about this tiny scratch on the floor when I moved in, took like five pictures of it on his iPad. He’s one of those guys who acts like he’s running a luxury hotel when it’s just a crappy one-bedroom apartment with paper-thin walls. I can hear the guy upstairs walking around all night, it sounds like he’s wearing bowling shoes... \n\nBut, uh, anyway, no, I wouldn't say my emotions are out of control. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these problems with staying on track and feeling restless?\n\nPatient: It's been forever, honestly. Like, even back in elementary school, I remember my teachers were always moving my desk around. They'd put me right next to their desk, or in the back corner, because I was always talking out of turn or forgetting to bring my homework in. And whenever we had worksheets, I'd just blast through them and make all these stupid careless mistakes. \n\nSpeaking of elementary school, they just tore down my old school last month. It's so weird driving by there now and it's just this massive pile of dirt and bricks. They're supposed to be building some kind of mixed-use development, you know, with the overpriced apartments on top and the empty retail spaces on the bottom? Every town is building those now. It just looks so generic. But anyway, yeah, I've always been like this. It's not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless are causing problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. It's actually really frustrating because I want to get my life going, you know? I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work, I'm great with cars. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I mess up the paperwork and have to redo the applications. So I'm just kind of stuck and not moving forward. \n\nWhich sucks because I really want to get into a good shop. My uncle used to run a garage out by the old highway, and I spent practically my whole summer there when I was fourteen. He had this classic 1969 Mustang Mach 1 he was restoring. It was originally that bright grabber blue, but the guy who owned it before him tried to paint it black himself in his driveway, and it looked terrible. My uncle spent weeks just sanding that thing down. He used to let me sort the spark plugs and clean the tools. He always said you could tell a good mechanic by how clean they kept their wrenches. He had this whole pegboard system that was perfectly outlined with a sharpie so you knew exactly where everything went. I should probably do that in my own apartment, honestly, maybe I wouldn't lose my keys every single morning... \n\nBut yeah, I’m just stuck right now. It definitely gets in the way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or following instructions in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, school was pretty rough. Following instructions was always hard because I just couldn't keep my focus on what the teacher was saying. Even now, like, I can start watching a repair video or reading the manual for a new tool, but after a few minutes I realize I’ve just been staring at it and I have to back it up because none of it stuck. \n\nIt's like when I was trying to figure out how to use this new OBD2 scanner I bought. It was supposed to be Bluetooth, right? You hook it up to your phone so you can read the engine codes without having to use the clunky handheld ones. I got it on Amazon, one of those lightning deals they always have. I swear, I spend way too much time looking at those deals. Last week I almost bought a set of mixing bowls just because they were 40% off, and I don't even bake. I don't even know where I'd put them, my kitchen cabinets are already full of random junk I never use. I have a panini press I used exactly once, like three years ago, and now it just takes up an entire shelf... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, school. Yeah, I just drifted off a lot. Still do, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were you more active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was definitely one of those kids who couldn't sit still. I played a ton of soccer back then, which was great because it burned off all that extra energy. I was on this travel team for a few years, we actually went all the way to a tournament in Florida once. It was August, though, so it was ridiculously hot. Like, the kind of heat where the air feels heavy and you're sweating before you even get out of the car. We went to this alligator park on one of our days off, and I swear those things didn't even look real. They just sat there completely motionless in the mud. I don't know how they do it. \n\nBut yeah, I don't play soccer anymore, and it's been driving me crazy. Sitting around the house now, I just get this feeling like I'm crawling out of my skin. I literally end up just pacing from room to room because I can't stand just sitting there doing nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty staying on track or your restlessness causes you more problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's probably the getting distracted part. The main thing is I get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. Like, I’ll be trying to work on an application for a shop, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone buzzes, or I just get some random idea, and suddenly I'm doing something completely different before I even notice. \n\nIt happens all the time with my phone. I'll pick it up just to check the time, and then I see a notification from YouTube about a video on how to rebuild a carburetor. Next thing I know, I've spent two hours watching videos about vintage motorcycles. I don't even own a motorcycle. I've always wanted one, though. My buddy Dave has this sick Triumph Bonneville, it's matte black with these custom leather saddlebags. He let me ride it once in an empty parking lot, and the torque on that thing is insane. You just barely twist the throttle and it wants to fly out from under you. He actually dropped it a couple weeks ago, scratched up the exhaust pipe pretty bad. I told him he could probably buff most of it out with some rubbing compound, but he's super paranoid about messing up the finish... \n\nBut yeah, the distraction thing. That's definitely the biggest problem for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with distractibility and restlessness as a young child, before you started school?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. Even in elementary school, it was always a thing. I remember my teachers were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever was next to me. I'd always forget my homework, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets and make all these stupid, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done with it. \n\nI remember one time in third grade, Mrs. Gable—she was this older lady who always smelled like peppermint and hairspray—she moved my desk right next to hers. She had this massive jar of peppermints on her desk, the kind with the red and white stripes. I actually hate peppermint. It reminds me of toothpaste. I don't get why people like mint chocolate, either. It's like brushing your teeth and eating a candy bar at the same time. My mom used to buy mint chocolate chip ice cream all the time because it was her favorite, and I’d always have to eat around the little chocolate flakes because the mint part was just gross. She’d get the big gallon tubs from Costco... man, Costco is dangerous. You go in for paper towels and come out with a kayak and a 50-pound bag of rice. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, being a little kid. Yeah, it’s always been like that, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus and stay on track if you were doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: Well, you'd think so, right? Like, if I'm really into something, I should be able to just lock in. But honestly, even with stuff I really care about, it's a struggle. Like, I love fixing things, right? So I'll start watching a repair video or I'll pull out the manual for a new tool. But after just a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and none of it actually stuck. I have to keep backing the video up or re-reading the same paragraph over and over. \n\nIt's so frustrating, especially with those repair videos. Some of those guys on YouTube are great, but others just talk way too much before they even get to the point. Like, there's this one channel I watch sometimes, and the guy always spends the first ten minutes talking about the history of the engine block or something. I mean, it's cool if you're a history buff, but I just want to know how to set the timing belt. My dad used to be like that, actually. You'd ask him how to fix a leaky faucet, and he'd start explaining the municipal water system. He worked for the city water department for like thirty years. He always had these crazy stories about the stuff they'd find in the sewers. Once they found a whole... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, focusing on stuff I like. Yeah, even then, my brain just kind of checks out after a few minutes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking or finish their sentences for them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do that a lot. It's really bad with my mom, or like, when I was talking to the career counselor last week. They'll be in the middle of asking me something, and the answer just pops into my head, and I just blurt it out before they even finish their sentence. It's like I can't hold it in. \n\nThe counselor was actually trying to help me figure out some stuff for this warehouse job, which was cool of him, but I kept cutting him off. He had this really slow way of talking, you know? Like every word was a struggle to get out. It reminded me of my uncle Pete. Pete talks so slow you could take a nap between his sentences. He used to be a trucker, drove big rigs all over the country. He actually brought me a real cowboy hat from Texas once when I was a kid. It was way too big for me, made me look like a mushroom, but I wore it everywhere. I even tried to wear it to bed once, but my mom made me take it off because she said it was dirty. She was always weird about germs. Like, she wouldn't even let me eat food if it fell on the floor for like half a second. The five-second rule didn't exist in our house. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I interrupt people a lot. I don't mean to be rude, it just comes out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Yeah, it definitely causes problems. My mom gets so annoyed with me because I'm always cutting her off or losing track of what she's saying. But honestly, the bigger issue right now is just getting my life together. Like, I really want to be an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work—I'm good with cars. I went to one of those derbies once at the county fair. The noise was insane, just metal crunching everywhere. They had these giant turkey legs at the fair, too. I don't know how they cook them, but they always taste like ham. Have you ever noticed that? Turkey legs at fairs just taste like salty ham. I tried to make one at home once, but it just came out dry. My mom told me I didn't brine it long enough, but who has time to let a piece of meat sit in salt water for two days? 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, problems with relationships and stuff. Yeah, it's frustrating. I'm just not moving forward with the mechanic stuff because I keep messing up the paperwork and deadlines.

Yeah, all the time. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were constantly moving my desk around. We used to drive that thing out to the lake back in high school and the radio only played cassettes... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just end up somewhere else entirely and I can't stay on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, constantly. Like, if I'm sitting down with someone, my leg is almost always bouncing up and down. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just digging in my pocket messing with my keys, and I don't even realize I'm doing it until someone points it out. My ex used to hate the pen clicking thing. She’d actually reach over and snatch it out of my hand if we were trying to watch a movie. We used to watch a lot of those true crime documentaries, you know? The ones where they stretch it out into like ten episodes even though they could have told the whole story in two hours. I can't stand when they do that, just dragging out the same interviews over and over... but yeah, I definitely can't just sit still. I'm always moving something.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I do things without thinking. I actually think about stuff a lot before I do it. Like if I'm going to buy something, I'll usually hold off and think on it. \n\nExcept, man, everything is just so expensive right now anyway, so you kind of have to wait. I went to get a burger yesterday and a basic combo meal was like fifteen bucks. Can you believe that? For a drive-thru! It used to be you could get a whole meal for five dollars. I remember back in high school me and my friends would go to Wendy's after we got out and we'd get so much food with just the crumpled up cash we had in our pockets. We used to hang out in that parking lot for hours just talking about whatever. The manager hated us being there, but he never actually kicked us out... \n\nBut yeah, what were we saying? Impulsive? No, I don't really just jump into things like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble controlling your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think so. I mean, I get mad sometimes, sure, but I don't think I fly off the handle or anything. I don't really yell at people. Usually, if I'm upset, I just kind of keep it to myself or try to distract myself. \n\nLike the other day, I was trying to fix this leaky pipe under my sink, and I just could not get the wrench to grip the stupid bolt. I was getting so frustrated I actually threw the wrench across the kitchen. It dented the drywall right next to the fridge, which sucks because I'm renting and my landlord is already super strict about the security deposit. He made a huge deal about this tiny scratch on the floor when I moved in, took like five pictures of it on his iPad. He’s one of those guys who acts like he’s running a luxury hotel when it’s just a crappy one-bedroom apartment with paper-thin walls. I can hear the guy upstairs walking around all night, it sounds like he’s wearing bowling shoes... \n\nBut, uh, anyway, no, I wouldn't say my emotions are out of control. Just normal stuff, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these problems with staying on track and feeling restless?\n\nPatient: It's been forever, honestly. Like, even back in elementary school, I remember my teachers were always moving my desk around. They'd put me right next to their desk, or in the back corner, because I was always talking out of turn or forgetting to bring my homework in. And whenever we had worksheets, I'd just blast through them and make all these stupid careless mistakes. \n\nSpeaking of elementary school, they just tore down my old school last month. It's so weird driving by there now and it's just this massive pile of dirt and bricks. They're supposed to be building some kind of mixed-use development, you know, with the overpriced apartments on top and the empty retail spaces on the bottom? Every town is building those now. It just looks so generic. But anyway, yeah, I've always been like this. It's not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless are causing problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. It's actually really frustrating because I want to get my life going, you know? I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work, I'm great with cars. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I mess up the paperwork and have to redo the applications. So I'm just kind of stuck and not moving forward. \n\nWhich sucks because I really want to get into a good shop. My uncle used to run a garage out by the old highway, and I spent practically my whole summer there when I was fourteen. He had this classic 1969 Mustang Mach 1 he was restoring. It was originally that bright grabber blue, but the guy who owned it before him tried to paint it black himself in his driveway, and it looked terrible. My uncle spent weeks just sanding that thing down. He used to let me sort the spark plugs and clean the tools. He always said you could tell a good mechanic by how clean they kept their wrenches. He had this whole pegboard system that was perfectly outlined with a sharpie so you knew exactly where everything went. I should probably do that in my own apartment, honestly, maybe I wouldn't lose my keys every single morning... \n\nBut yeah, I’m just stuck right now. It definitely gets in the way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or following instructions in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, school was pretty rough. Following instructions was always hard because I just couldn't keep my focus on what the teacher was saying. Even now, like, I can start watching a repair video or reading the manual for a new tool, but after a few minutes I realize I’ve just been staring at it and I have to back it up because none of it stuck. \n\nIt's like when I was trying to figure out how to use this new OBD2 scanner I bought. It was supposed to be Bluetooth, right? You hook it up to your phone so you can read the engine codes without having to use the clunky handheld ones. I got it on Amazon, one of those lightning deals they always have. I swear, I spend way too much time looking at those deals. Last week I almost bought a set of mixing bowls just because they were 40% off, and I don't even bake. I don't even know where I'd put them, my kitchen cabinets are already full of random junk I never use. I have a panini press I used exactly once, like three years ago, and now it just takes up an entire shelf... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, school. Yeah, I just drifted off a lot. Still do, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were you more active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was definitely one of those kids who couldn't sit still. I played a ton of soccer back then, which was great because it burned off all that extra energy. I was on this travel team for a few years, we actually went all the way to a tournament in Florida once. It was August, though, so it was ridiculously hot. Like, the kind of heat where the air feels heavy and you're sweating before you even get out of the car. We went to this alligator park on one of our days off, and I swear those things didn't even look real. They just sat there completely motionless in the mud. I don't know how they do it. \n\nBut yeah, I don't play soccer anymore, and it's been driving me crazy. Sitting around the house now, I just get this feeling like I'm crawling out of my skin. I literally end up just pacing from room to room because I can't stand just sitting there doing nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty staying on track or your restlessness causes you more problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's probably the getting distracted part. The main thing is I get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. Like, I’ll be trying to work on an application for a shop, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone buzzes, or I just get some random idea, and suddenly I'm doing something completely different before I even notice. \n\nIt happens all the time with my phone. I'll pick it up just to check the time, and then I see a notification from YouTube about a video on how to rebuild a carburetor. Next thing I know, I've spent two hours watching videos about vintage motorcycles. I don't even own a motorcycle. I've always wanted one, though. My buddy Dave has this sick Triumph Bonneville, it's matte black with these custom leather saddlebags. He let me ride it once in an empty parking lot, and the torque on that thing is insane. You just barely twist the throttle and it wants to fly out from under you. He actually dropped it a couple weeks ago, scratched up the exhaust pipe pretty bad. I told him he could probably buff most of it out with some rubbing compound, but he's super paranoid about messing up the finish... \n\nBut yeah, the distraction thing. That's definitely the biggest problem for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with distractibility and restlessness as a young child, before you started school?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. Even in elementary school, it was always a thing. I remember my teachers were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever was next to me. I'd always forget my homework, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets and make all these stupid, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done with it. \n\nI remember one time in third grade, Mrs. Gable—she was this older lady who always smelled like peppermint and hairspray—she moved my desk right next to hers. She had this massive jar of peppermints on her desk, the kind with the red and white stripes. I actually hate peppermint. It reminds me of toothpaste. I don't get why people like mint chocolate, either. It's like brushing your teeth and eating a candy bar at the same time. My mom used to buy mint chocolate chip ice cream all the time because it was her favorite, and I’d always have to eat around the little chocolate flakes because the mint part was just gross. She’d get the big gallon tubs from Costco... man, Costco is dangerous. You go in for paper towels and come out with a kayak and a 50-pound bag of rice. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, being a little kid. Yeah, it’s always been like that, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus and stay on track if you were doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: Well, you'd think so, right? Like, if I'm really into something, I should be able to just lock in. But honestly, even with stuff I really care about, it's a struggle. Like, I love fixing things, right? So I'll start watching a repair video or I'll pull out the manual for a new tool. But after just a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and none of it actually stuck. I have to keep backing the video up or re-reading the same paragraph over and over. \n\nIt's so frustrating, especially with those repair videos. Some of those guys on YouTube are great, but others just talk way too much before they even get to the point. Like, there's this one channel I watch sometimes, and the guy always spends the first ten minutes talking about the history of the engine block or something. I mean, it's cool if you're a history buff, but I just want to know how to set the timing belt. My dad used to be like that, actually. You'd ask him how to fix a leaky faucet, and he'd start explaining the municipal water system. He worked for the city water department for like thirty years. He always had these crazy stories about the stuff they'd find in the sewers. Once they found a whole... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, focusing on stuff I like. It's like I can't hold it in. \n\nThe counselor was actually trying to help me figure out some stuff for this warehouse job, which was cool of him, but I kept cutting him off. He used to be a trucker, drove big rigs all over the country. He actually brought me a real cowboy hat from Texas once when I was a kid. It was way too big for me, made me look like a mushroom, but I wore it everywhere. I even tried to wear it to bed once, but my mom made me take it off because she said it was dirty. She was always weird about germs. Like, she wouldn't even let me eat food if it fell on the floor for like half a second. The five-second rule didn't exist in our house. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I interrupt people a lot. I don't mean to be rude, it just comes out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your difficulties with staying on track, restlessness, or interrupting others have caused problems in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. I tried to make one at home once, but it just came out dry. I went to one of those derbies once at the county fair. The noise was insane, just metal crunching everywhere. They had these giant turkey legs at the fair, too. I don't know how they cook them, but they always taste like ham. Have you ever noticed that? Turkey legs at fairs just taste like salty ham. I tried to make one at home once, but it just came out dry. My mom told me I didn't brine it long enough, but who has time to let a piece of meat sit in salt water for two days? \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, problems with relationships and stuff. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, not really, no. I mean, I guess I can't really think of anything where I just sit there and get completely lost in it. I'm usually bouncing around too much. Like, even when I'm trying to play video games, I'll play for maybe ten minutes and then I have to get up and get a snack or check my phone. 

Speaking of my phone, I need to get the screen fixed. I dropped it on the driveway yesterday when I was trying to carry in like, six bags of groceries at once. You know when you just refuse to make two trips? I had bags looped up to my elbows, and the one with the milk started slipping, so I tried to juggle it, and boom, phone right on the concrete. It's totally shattered. It's an older phone anyway, though, so maybe I should just get a new one. But they're so expensive now. My buddy Dave—the one with the Civic—he just got that new one with the three cameras on the back. It takes really good pictures, especially in the dark. We went to this bonfire last weekend out by the lake, and he got this awesome picture of the sparks flying up into the sky. It was a good fire, too. We used this old pallet wood we found behind a strip mall. It burned super hot. Some guy brought a guitar, but he only knew how to play "Wonderwall," which was kind of annoying after the third time. 

But yeah... I don't really get hyper-focused on stuff. I'm pretty much always all over the place.

It's so weird driving by there now and it's just this massive pile of dirt and bricks. They're supposed to be building some kind of mixed-use development, you know, with the overpriced apartments on top and the empty retail spaces on the bottom? Every town is building those now. It just looks so generic. But anyway, yeah, I've always been like this. It's not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless are causing problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. It's actually really frustrating because I want to get my life going, you know? I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work, I'm great with cars. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I mess up the paperwork and have to redo the applications. So I'm just kind of stuck and not moving forward. \n\nWhich sucks because I really want to get into a good shop. My uncle used to run a garage out by the old highway, and I spent practically my whole summer there when I was fourteen. He had this classic 1969 Mustang Mach 1 he was restoring. It was originally that bright grabber blue, but the guy who owned it before him tried to paint it black himself in his driveway, and it looked terrible. My uncle spent weeks just sanding that thing down. He used to let me sort the spark plugs and clean the tools. He always said you could tell a good mechanic by how clean they kept their wrenches. He had this whole pegboard system that was perfectly outlined with a sharpie so you knew exactly where everything went. I should probably do that in my own apartment, honestly, maybe I wouldn't lose my keys every single morning... \n\nBut yeah, I’m just stuck right now. It definitely gets in the way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or following instructions in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, school was pretty rough. Following instructions was always hard because I just couldn't keep my focus on what the teacher was saying. Even now, like, I can start watching a repair video or reading the manual for a new tool, but after a few minutes I realize I’ve just been staring at it and I have to back it up because none of it stuck. \n\nIt's like when I was trying to figure out how to use this new OBD2 scanner I bought. It was supposed to be Bluetooth, right? You hook it up to your phone so you can read the engine codes without having to use the clunky handheld ones. I got it on Amazon, one of those lightning deals they always have. I swear, I spend way too much time looking at those deals. Last week I almost bought a set of mixing bowls just because they were 40% off, and I don't even bake. I don't even know where I'd put them, my kitchen cabinets are already full of random junk I never use. I have a panini press I used exactly once, like three years ago, and now it just takes up an entire shelf... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, school. Yeah, I just drifted off a lot. Still do, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were you more active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was definitely one of those kids who couldn't sit still. I played a ton of soccer back then, which was great because it burned off all that extra energy. I was on this travel team for a few years, we actually went all the way to a tournament in Florida once. It was August, though, so it was ridiculously hot. Like, the kind of heat where the air feels heavy and you're sweating before you even get out of the car. We went to this alligator park on one of our days off, and I swear those things didn't even look real. They just sat there completely motionless in the mud. I don't know how they do it. \n\nBut yeah, I don't play soccer anymore, and it's been driving me crazy. Sitting around the house now, I just get this feeling like I'm crawling out of my skin. I literally end up just pacing from room to room because I can't stand just sitting there doing nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty staying on track or your restlessness causes you more problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's probably the getting distracted part. The main thing is I get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. Like, I’ll be trying to work on an application for a shop, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone buzzes, or I just get some random idea, and suddenly I'm doing something completely different before I even notice. \n\nIt happens all the time with my phone. I'll pick it up just to check the time, and then I see a notification from YouTube about a video on how to rebuild a carburetor. Next thing I know, I've spent two hours watching videos about vintage motorcycles. I don't even own a motorcycle. I've always wanted one, though. My buddy Dave has this sick Triumph Bonneville, it's matte black with these custom leather saddlebags. He let me ride it once in an empty parking lot, and the torque on that thing is insane. You just barely twist the throttle and it wants to fly out from under you. He actually dropped it a couple weeks ago, scratched up the exhaust pipe pretty bad. I told him he could probably buff most of it out with some rubbing compound, but he's super paranoid about messing up the finish... \n\nBut yeah, the distraction thing. That's definitely the biggest problem for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with distractibility and restlessness as a young child, before you started school?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. Even in elementary school, it was always a thing. I remember my teachers were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever was next to me. I'd always forget my homework, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets and make all these stupid, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done with it. \n\nI remember one time in third grade, Mrs. Gable—she was this older lady who always smelled like peppermint and hairspray—she moved my desk right next to hers. She had this massive jar of peppermints on her desk, the kind with the red and white stripes. I actually hate peppermint. It reminds me of toothpaste. I don't get why people like mint chocolate, either. It's like brushing your teeth and eating a candy bar at the same time. My mom used to buy mint chocolate chip ice cream all the time because it was her favorite, and I’d always have to eat around the little chocolate flakes because the mint part was just gross. She’d get the big gallon tubs from Costco... man, Costco is dangerous. You go in for paper towels and come out with a kayak and a 50-pound bag of rice. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, being a little kid. Yeah, it’s always been like that, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus and stay on track if you were doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: Well, you'd think so, right? Like, if I'm really into something, I should be able to just lock in. But honestly, even with stuff I really care about, it's a struggle. Like, I love fixing things, right? So I'll start watching a repair video or I'll pull out the manual for a new tool. But after just a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and none of it actually stuck. I have to keep backing the video up or re-reading the same paragraph over and over. \n\nIt's so frustrating, especially with those repair videos. Some of those guys on YouTube are great, but others just talk way too much before they even get to the point. Like, there's this one channel I watch sometimes, and the guy always spends the first ten minutes talking about the history of the engine block or something. I mean, it's cool if you're a history buff, but I just want to know how to set the timing belt. My dad used to be like that, actually. You'd ask him how to fix a leaky faucet, and he'd start explaining the municipal water system. He worked for the city water department for like thirty years. He always had these crazy stories about the stuff they'd find in the sewers. Once they found a whole... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, focusing on stuff I like. Yeah, even then, my brain just kind of checks out after a few minutes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking or finish their sentences for them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do that a lot. It's really bad with my mom, or like, when I was talking to the career counselor last week. They'll be in the middle of asking me something, and the answer just pops into my head, and I just blurt it out before they even finish their sentence. It's like I can't hold it in. \n\nThe counselor was actually trying to help me figure out some stuff for this warehouse job, which was cool of him, but I kept cutting him off. He had this really slow way of talking, you know? Like every word was a struggle to get out. It reminded me of my uncle Pete. Pete talks so slow you could take a nap between his sentences. He used to be a trucker, drove big rigs all over the country. He actually brought me a real cowboy hat from Texas once when I was a kid. It was way too big for me, made me look like a mushroom, but I wore it everywhere. I even tried to wear it to bed once, but my mom made me take it off because she said it was dirty. She was always weird about germs. Like, she wouldn't even let me eat food if it fell on the floor for like half a second. The five-second rule didn't exist in our house. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I interrupt people a lot. I don't mean to be rude, it just comes out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your difficulties with staying on track, restlessness, or interrupting others have caused problems in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. My mom gets so annoyed with me because I'm always cutting her off or losing track of what she's saying. But honestly, the bigger issue right now is just getting my life together. Like, I really want to be an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work—I'm good with cars. But I've missed so many training deadlines, and I've had to redo applications so many times because I forget a form or miss a step. I'm just stuck. \n\nI've always liked cars, though. My buddy Dave had this beat-up '98 Civic in high school, and we used to spend hours trying to fix it up. We thought we were hot stuff because we put a spoiler on it and painted the brake calipers red. It looked ridiculous, honestly. Dave actually ended up selling that car to a guy who turned it into a demolition derby car. Can you imagine? A tiny little Civic in a demolition derby. I went to one of those derbies once at the county fair. The noise was insane, just metal crunching everywhere. They had these giant turkey legs at the fair, too. I don't know how they cook them, but they always taste like ham. Have you ever noticed that? Turkey legs at fairs just taste like salty ham. I tried to make one at home once, but it just came out dry. My mom told me I didn't brine it long enough, but who has time to let a piece of meat sit in salt water for two days? \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, problems with relationships and stuff. Yeah, it's frustrating. I'm just not moving forward with the mechanic stuff because I keep messing up the paperwork and deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Did your parents or teachers ever comment on your behavior or attention span when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were constantly moving my desk around. Like, they'd put me right next to the teacher's desk, or in the very back corner, because I was always talking out of turn or forgetting my homework. I'd rush through worksheets just to get them done, and I'd make all these stupid, careless mistakes. \n\nI remember Mrs. Gable in third grade—she had this giant jar of marbles on her desk. Every time the class was good, she'd drop a marble in, and if we filled it, we got a pizza party. But she'd always threaten to take marbles out if I didn't sit still. She was really strict, but she smelled like peppermint all the time. I actually love peppermint. Especially those little red and white swirly candies. My grandma always had a bowl of those in her living room, right next to this creepy porcelain doll she got at a flea market. The doll had these glass eyes that literally followed you around the room. I swear it was haunted. My cousin Joey tried to hide it in the closet once, and my grandma chased him out of the house with a wooden spoon. Joey was always getting into trouble, though. He once tried to build a ramp for his skateboard out of some old plywood and a milk crate, and he ended up breaking his collarbone. He had to wear this weird harness thing for like six weeks... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, teachers and stuff. Yeah, they were always on my case about rushing through work and talking too much.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you become so focused that you lose track of time and forget about everything else?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. I mean, I guess I can't really think of anything where I just sit there and get completely lost in it. I'm usually bouncing around too much. Like, even when I'm trying to play video games, I'll play for maybe ten minutes and then I have to get up and get a snack or check my phone. \n\nSpeaking of my phone, I need to get the screen fixed. I dropped it on the driveway yesterday when I was trying to carry in like, six bags of groceries at once. You know when you just refuse to make two trips? I had bags looped up to my elbows, and the one with the milk started slipping, so I tried to juggle it, and boom, phone right on the concrete. It's totally shattered. It's an older phone anyway, though, so maybe I should just get a new one. But they're so expensive now. My buddy Dave—the one with the Civic—he just got that new one with the three cameras on the back. It takes really good pictures, especially in the dark. We went to this bonfire last weekend out by the lake, and he got this awesome picture of the sparks flying up into the sky. It was a good fire, too. We used this old pallet wood we found behind a strip mall. It burned super hot. Some guy brought a guitar, but he only knew how to play \"Wonderwall,\" which was kind of annoying after the third time. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really get hyper-focused on stuff. I'm pretty much always all over the place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's an older phone anyway, though, so maybe I should just get a new one. But they're so expensive now. My buddy Dave—the one with the Civic—he just got that new one with the three cameras on the back. It takes really good pictures, especially in the dark. We went to this bonfire last weekend out by the lake, and he got this awesome picture of the sparks flying up into the sky. It was a good fire, too. We used this old pallet wood we found behind a strip mall. It burned super hot. Some guy brought a guitar, but he only knew how to play \"Wonderwall,\" which was kind of annoying after the third time. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really get hyper-focused on stuff. I'm pretty much always all over the place.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless are more related to problems with organization and time management or with being easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / no_occupation / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Organization_Difficulty\nmanifestation: \"If I have a bunch of things to do, like call the counselor, clean up my room, and send in an application, I don’t know what to start with, so I bounce around and half-finish everything.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it's mostly the organization part. Like, if I have a bunch of things to do—say I need to call the career counselor, clean up my room, and finally send in that application for the auto tech program—I just look at it all and I have no idea where to start. So I end up bouncing around between them and just half-finishing everything. 

Like yesterday, I started cleaning my room because I couldn't find my favorite hoodie. It's this gray one with a grease stain on the sleeve that I can't get out. I've tried everything—dish soap, baking soda, even this weird industrial cleaner my uncle gave me. My uncle works at a dry cleaner, actually. He’s always smelling like those weird chemicals. He used to bring home these huge rolls of plastic garment bags, and when we were kids, my cousins and I would use them as slip-and-slides in the backyard. We'd just put the hose on them and go flying. One time I slid right off the plastic and into my mom’s rose bushes. Man, those thorns tore up my leg pretty bad. I still have a little scar on my shin from it. It's shaped kind of like a lightning bolt, which is pretty cool, honestly. Reminds me of Harry Potter. I tried reading those books once, but I could never get past the second one. The movies were okay, though. 

Anyway... yeah, I just can't figure out how to prioritize things. I just jump from one task to another and nothing ever actually gets done.

It's like I can't hold it in. \n\nThe counselor was actually trying to help me figure out some stuff for this warehouse job, which was cool of him, but I kept cutting him off. She was really strict, but she smelled like peppermint all the time. I actually love peppermint. Especially those little red and white swirly candies. My grandma always had a bowl of those in her living room, right next to this creepy porcelain doll she got at a flea market. The doll had these glass eyes that literally followed you around the room. I swear it was haunted. My cousin Joey tried to hide it in the closet once, and my grandma chased him out of the house with a wooden spoon. Joey was always getting into trouble, though. He once tried to build a ramp for his skateboard out of some old plywood and a milk crate, and he ended up breaking his collarbone. He had to wear this weird harness thing for like six weeks... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, teachers and stuff. Yeah, they were always on my case about rushing through work and talking too much.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you become so focused that you lose track of time and forget about everything else?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. Like, even when I'm trying to play video games, I'll play for maybe ten minutes and then I have to get up and get a snack or check my phone. \n\nSpeaking of my phone, I need to get the screen fixed. They'd put me right next to their desk, or in the back corner, because I was always talking out of turn or forgetting to bring my homework in. And whenever we had worksheets, I'd just blast through them and make all these stupid careless mistakes. \n\nSpeaking of elementary school, they just tore down my old school last month. It's so weird driving by there now and it's just this massive pile of dirt and bricks. They're supposed to be building some kind of mixed-use development, you know, with the overpriced apartments on top and the empty retail spaces on the bottom? Every town is building those now. It just looks so generic. But anyway, yeah, I've always been like this. It's not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless are causing problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. It's actually really frustrating because I want to get my life going, you know? I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work, I'm great with cars. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I mess up the paperwork and have to redo the applications. So I'm just kind of stuck and not moving forward. \n\nWhich sucks because I really want to get into a good shop. My uncle used to run a garage out by the old highway, and I spent practically my whole summer there when I was fourteen. He had this classic 1969 Mustang Mach 1 he was restoring. It was originally that bright grabber blue, but the guy who owned it before him tried to paint it black himself in his driveway, and it looked terrible. My uncle spent weeks just sanding that thing down. He used to let me sort the spark plugs and clean the tools. He always said you could tell a good mechanic by how clean they kept their wrenches. He had this whole pegboard system that was perfectly outlined with a sharpie so you knew exactly where everything went. I should probably do that in my own apartment, honestly, maybe I wouldn't lose my keys every single morning... \n\nBut yeah, I’m just stuck right now. It definitely gets in the way.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or following instructions in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, school was pretty rough. Following instructions was always hard because I just couldn't keep my focus on what the teacher was saying. Even now, like, I can start watching a repair video or reading the manual for a new tool, but after a few minutes I realize I’ve just been staring at it and I have to back it up because none of it stuck. \n\nIt's like when I was trying to figure out how to use this new OBD2 scanner I bought. It was supposed to be Bluetooth, right? You hook it up to your phone so you can read the engine codes without having to use the clunky handheld ones. I got it on Amazon, one of those lightning deals they always have. I swear, I spend way too much time looking at those deals. Last week I almost bought a set of mixing bowls just because they were 40% off, and I don't even bake. I don't even know where I'd put them, my kitchen cabinets are already full of random junk I never use. I have a panini press I used exactly once, like three years ago, and now it just takes up an entire shelf... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, school. Yeah, I just drifted off a lot. Still do, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Were you more active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was definitely one of those kids who couldn't sit still. I played a ton of soccer back then, which was great because it burned off all that extra energy. I was on this travel team for a few years, we actually went all the way to a tournament in Florida once. It was August, though, so it was ridiculously hot. Like, the kind of heat where the air feels heavy and you're sweating before you even get out of the car. We went to this alligator park on one of our days off, and I swear those things didn't even look real. They just sat there completely motionless in the mud. I don't know how they do it. \n\nBut yeah, I don't play soccer anymore, and it's been driving me crazy. Sitting around the house now, I just get this feeling like I'm crawling out of my skin. I literally end up just pacing from room to room because I can't stand just sitting there doing nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulty staying on track or your restlessness causes you more problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I think... well, it's probably the getting distracted part. The main thing is I get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. Like, I’ll be trying to work on an application for a shop, and then a car drives by outside, or my phone buzzes, or I just get some random idea, and suddenly I'm doing something completely different before I even notice. \n\nIt happens all the time with my phone. I'll pick it up just to check the time, and then I see a notification from YouTube about a video on how to rebuild a carburetor. Next thing I know, I've spent two hours watching videos about vintage motorcycles. I don't even own a motorcycle. I've always wanted one, though. My buddy Dave has this sick Triumph Bonneville, it's matte black with these custom leather saddlebags. He let me ride it once in an empty parking lot, and the torque on that thing is insane. You just barely twist the throttle and it wants to fly out from under you. He actually dropped it a couple weeks ago, scratched up the exhaust pipe pretty bad. I told him he could probably buff most of it out with some rubbing compound, but he's super paranoid about messing up the finish... \n\nBut yeah, the distraction thing. That's definitely the biggest problem for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with distractibility and restlessness as a young child, before you started school?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. Even in elementary school, it was always a thing. I remember my teachers were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever was next to me. I'd always forget my homework, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets and make all these stupid, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done with it. \n\nI remember one time in third grade, Mrs. Gable—she was this older lady who always smelled like peppermint and hairspray—she moved my desk right next to hers. She had this massive jar of peppermints on her desk, the kind with the red and white stripes. I actually hate peppermint. It reminds me of toothpaste. I don't get why people like mint chocolate, either. It's like brushing your teeth and eating a candy bar at the same time. My mom used to buy mint chocolate chip ice cream all the time because it was her favorite, and I’d always have to eat around the little chocolate flakes because the mint part was just gross. She’d get the big gallon tubs from Costco... man, Costco is dangerous. You go in for paper towels and come out with a kayak and a 50-pound bag of rice. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, being a little kid. Yeah, it’s always been like that, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus and stay on track if you were doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: Well, you'd think so, right? Like, if I'm really into something, I should be able to just lock in. But honestly, even with stuff I really care about, it's a struggle. Like, I love fixing things, right? So I'll start watching a repair video or I'll pull out the manual for a new tool. But after just a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and none of it actually stuck. I have to keep backing the video up or re-reading the same paragraph over and over. \n\nIt's so frustrating, especially with those repair videos. Some of those guys on YouTube are great, but others just talk way too much before they even get to the point. Like, there's this one channel I watch sometimes, and the guy always spends the first ten minutes talking about the history of the engine block or something. I mean, it's cool if you're a history buff, but I just want to know how to set the timing belt. My dad used to be like that, actually. You'd ask him how to fix a leaky faucet, and he'd start explaining the municipal water system. He worked for the city water department for like thirty years. He always had these crazy stories about the stuff they'd find in the sewers. Once they found a whole... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, focusing on stuff I like. Yeah, even then, my brain just kind of checks out after a few minutes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking or finish their sentences for them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do that a lot. It's really bad with my mom, or like, when I was talking to the career counselor last week. They'll be in the middle of asking me something, and the answer just pops into my head, and I just blurt it out before they even finish their sentence. It's like I can't hold it in. \n\nThe counselor was actually trying to help me figure out some stuff for this warehouse job, which was cool of him, but I kept cutting him off. He had this really slow way of talking, you know? Like every word was a struggle to get out. It reminded me of my uncle Pete. Pete talks so slow you could take a nap between his sentences. He used to be a trucker, drove big rigs all over the country. He actually brought me a real cowboy hat from Texas once when I was a kid. It was way too big for me, made me look like a mushroom, but I wore it everywhere. I even tried to wear it to bed once, but my mom made me take it off because she said it was dirty. She was always weird about germs. Like, she wouldn't even let me eat food if it fell on the floor for like half a second. The five-second rule didn't exist in our house. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I interrupt people a lot. I don't know how they cook them, but they always taste like ham. Have you ever noticed that? Turkey legs at fairs just taste like salty ham. I tried to make one at home once, but it just came out dry. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, all the time. It's really frustrating, actually. Like, I'll be trying to figure something out—say I start watching a repair video on YouTube, or I'm trying to read the manual for a new tool I just bought. I'll be looking right at it, but after a few minutes, I realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and my mind is completely somewhere else. I was actually trying to fix my toaster last week. It's one of those retro-looking ones, chrome, really shiny. I got it at a thrift store for like five bucks. Thrift stores are great, honestly. I tried airing it out on the porch for like three days, but a stray cat ended up sleeping on it, so I just threw it away. That cat still hangs around my porch, actually. I call him Barnaby. He's missing half of his left ear, looks like he's been in some serious scraps. I bought a bag of cat food for him, the kind with the little fish shapes, but he only eats the red ones. I don't know why, maybe they taste different? I should probably look that up. 

But yeah, the toaster. I was watching a video on how to take the heating element out, and I just totally zoned out. I ended up watching like three minutes of the guy talking before I realized I was thinking about what kind of sandwich I wanted to make. I had to rewind it like four times just to get through one step.
