Like yesterday, I heard this really loud muffler outside, and it reminded me of my cousin's old Honda. We used to drive that thing out to the lake back in high school and the radio only played cassettes... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I just end up somewhere else entirely and I can't stay on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, constantly. Like, if I'm sitting down with someone, my leg is almost always bouncing up and down. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just digging in my pocket messing with my keys, and I don't even realize I'm doing it until someone points it out. My ex used to hate the pen clicking thing. She’d actually reach over and snatch it out of my hand if we were trying to watch a movie. We used to watch a lot of those true crime documentaries, you know? The ones where they stretch it out into like ten episodes even though they could have told the whole story in two hours. I can't stand when they do that, just dragging out the same interviews over and over... but yeah, I definitely can't just sit still. I'm always moving something.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Um, not really? I don't think I do things without thinking. I actually think about stuff a lot before I do it. Like if I'm going to buy something, I'll usually hold off and think on it. \n\nExcept, man, everything is just so expensive right now anyway, so you kind of have to wait. I went to get a burger yesterday and a basic combo meal was like fifteen bucks. Can you believe that? For a drive-thru! It used to be you could get a whole meal for five dollars. I remember back in high school me and my friends would go to Wendy's after we got out and we'd get so much food with just the crumpled up cash we had in our pockets. We used to hang out in that parking lot for hours just talking about whatever. The manager hated us being there, but he never actually kicked us out... \n\nBut yeah, what were we saying? Impulsive? No, I don't really just jump into things like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble controlling your emotions?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think so. I mean, I get mad sometimes, sure, but I don't think I fly off the handle or anything. I don't really yell at people. Usually, if I'm upset, I just kind of keep it to myself or try to distract myself. \n\nLike the other day, I was trying to fix this leaky pipe under my sink, and I just could not get the wrench to grip the stupid bolt. It's actually really frustrating because I want to get my life going, you know? I'm trying to become an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work, I'm great with cars. But I keep missing these training deadlines, or I mess up the paperwork and have to redo the applications. So I'm just kind of stuck and not moving forward. \n\nWhich sucks because I really want to get into a good shop. Even in elementary school, it was always a thing. I remember my teachers were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever was next to me. I'd always forget my homework, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets and make all these stupid, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done with it. \n\nI remember one time in third grade, Mrs. Gable—she was this older lady who always smelled like peppermint and hairspray—she moved my desk right next to hers. I don't get why people like mint chocolate, either. It's like brushing your teeth and eating a candy bar at the same time. Yeah, I mean, as far back as I can remember, honestly. It's not like a new thing. Even back in elementary school, it was always the same story. My teachers were constantly moving my seat because I wouldn't stop talking out of turn, or I'd forget my homework, or I'd just blow through the worksheets and make a ton of careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done. 

I remember my third-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable. She moved my desk right next to hers, facing the chalkboard, so I couldn't look at anyone else. He used to take it fishing with him. We'd go out to this little pond near his house, catch bluegill mostly. They're super bony, not really good for eating, but it was fun to catch them. We used live worms, and I remember feeling bad for them when we put them on the hook. My grandpa told me they didn't feel it, but I don't know if I believe that now. Worms are pretty complex, right? Like, if you cut them in half, they turn into two worms. Or is that a myth? I saw a documentary once about these deep-sea worms that live near hydrothermal vents. They look like giant tubes of lipstick. Nature is crazy like that. 

But yeah, the school stuff. It's just always been like this. I've always just been kind of... all over the place.

Like, if I have a bunch of things to do—say I need to call the career counselor, clean up my room, and finally send in that application for the auto tech program—I just look at it all and I have no idea where to start. Yeah, pretty much everywhere. It's not just a school thing or whatever. Like, at home, I can't even get through a simple chore without getting sidetracked, and hanging out with friends is just as bad. I'll be in the middle of a conversation and suddenly I'm just... not there. I get fidgety, or I'll interrupt people because if I don't say what's in my head right then, I'll completely forget it. 

I did that to my buddy Mark the other day. We were at this diner downtown, the one with the really good milkshakes. They serve them in those big metal cups, you know? So you get the glass and then the extra in the tin. I always get strawberry. It's weird because I hate actual strawberries—the texture of the seeds just bothers me—but artificial strawberry flavor is the best. It's like banana flavor. Did you know the artificial banana flavor is based on a type of banana that went extinct in the 1950s? The Gros Michel, I think it was called. Now we eat Cavendish bananas, but the candy still tastes like the old ones. It's wild that we're basically tasting a ghost fruit. I read this whole article about a fungus that wiped them out, and now the same fungus is threatening the bananas we have now. We might not even have bananas in like, twenty years. That would be crazy. I wonder what they'd replace them with in smoothies. Mangoes, maybe? Mangoes are pretty good, but they're a pain to cut. You always lose half the fruit trying to get around that massive pit in the middle. 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, Mark. Yeah, I kept cutting him off while he was talking about his car troubles because I remembered I needed to buy wiper fluid, and I just blurted it out. He got pretty annoyed. So yeah, it definitely happens everywhere.

You just barely twist the throttle and it wants to fly out from under you. He actually dropped it a couple weeks ago, scratched up the exhaust pipe pretty bad. That's definitely the biggest problem for me right now.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble with distractibility and restlessness as a young child, before you started school?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. Even in elementary school, it was always a thing. I remember my teachers were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever was next to me. I'd always forget my homework, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets and make all these stupid, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done with it. \n\nI remember one time in third grade, Mrs. Gable—she was this older lady who always smelled like peppermint and hairspray—she moved my desk right next to hers. She had this massive jar of peppermints on her desk, the kind with the red and white stripes. I actually hate peppermint. It reminds me of toothpaste. I don't get why people like mint chocolate, either. It's like brushing your teeth and eating a candy bar at the same time. My mom used to buy mint chocolate chip ice cream all the time because it was her favorite, and I’d always have to eat around the little chocolate flakes because the mint part was just gross. She’d get the big gallon tubs from Costco... man, Costco is dangerous. You go in for paper towels and come out with a kayak and a 50-pound bag of rice. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, being a little kid. Yeah, it’s always been like that, as far back as I can remember.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be able to focus and stay on track if you were doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: Well, you'd think so, right? Like, if I'm really into something, I should be able to just lock in. But honestly, even with stuff I really care about, it's a struggle. Like, I love fixing things, right? So I'll start watching a repair video or I'll pull out the manual for a new tool. But after just a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and none of it actually stuck. I have to keep backing the video up or re-reading the same paragraph over and over. \n\nIt's so frustrating, especially with those repair videos. Some of those guys on YouTube are great, but others just talk way too much before they even get to the point. Like, there's this one channel I watch sometimes, and the guy always spends the first ten minutes talking about the history of the engine block or something. I mean, it's cool if you're a history buff, but I just want to know how to set the timing belt. My dad used to be like that, actually. You'd ask him how to fix a leaky faucet, and he'd start explaining the municipal water system. He worked for the city water department for like thirty years. He always had these crazy stories about the stuff they'd find in the sewers. Once they found a whole... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, focusing on stuff I like. Yeah, even then, my brain just kind of checks out after a few minutes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're talking or finish their sentences for them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do that a lot. It's really bad with my mom, or like, when I was talking to the career counselor last week. They'll be in the middle of asking me something, and the answer just pops into my head, and I just blurt it out before they even finish their sentence. It's like I can't hold it in. \n\nThe counselor was actually trying to help me figure out some stuff for this warehouse job, which was cool of him, but I kept cutting him off. He had this really slow way of talking, you know? Like every word was a struggle to get out. It reminded me of my uncle Pete. Pete talks so slow you could take a nap between his sentences. He used to be a trucker, drove big rigs all over the country. He actually brought me a real cowboy hat from Texas once when I was a kid. It was way too big for me, made me look like a mushroom, but I wore it everywhere. I even tried to wear it to bed once, but my mom made me take it off because she said it was dirty. She was always weird about germs. Like, she wouldn't even let me eat food if it fell on the floor for like half a second. The five-second rule didn't exist in our house. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I interrupt people a lot. I don't mean to be rude, it just comes out.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your difficulties with staying on track, restlessness, or interrupting others have caused problems in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely causes problems. Like, I really want to be an automotive technician. I know I can do the hands-on work—I'm good with cars. But I've missed so many training deadlines, and I've had to redo applications so many times because I forget a form or miss a step. I'm just stuck. \n\nI've always liked cars, though. I don't know how they cook them, but they always taste like ham. Have you ever noticed that? Turkey legs at fairs just taste like salty ham. I tried to make one at home once, but it just came out dry. Yeah, it's frustrating. I'm just not moving forward with the mechanic stuff because I keep messing up the paperwork and deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Did your parents or teachers ever comment on your behavior or attention span when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were constantly moving my desk around. Like, they'd put me right next to the teacher's desk, or in the very back corner, because I was always talking out of turn or forgetting my homework. I'd rush through worksheets just to get them done, and I'd make all these stupid, careless mistakes. \n\nI remember Mrs. Gable in third grade—she had this giant jar of marbles on her desk. Every time the class was good, she'd drop a marble in, and if we filled it, we got a pizza party. But she'd always threaten to take marbles out if I didn't sit still. She was really strict, but she smelled like peppermint all the time. I actually love peppermint. Especially those little red and white swirly candies. My grandma always had a bowl of those in her living room, right next to this creepy porcelain doll she got at a flea market. The doll had these glass eyes that literally followed you around the room. He once tried to build a ramp for his skateboard out of some old plywood and a milk crate, and he ended up breaking his collarbone. He had to wear this weird harness thing for like six weeks... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, teachers and stuff. Like, even when I'm trying to play video games, I'll play for maybe ten minutes and then I have to get up and get a snack or check my phone. \n\nSpeaking of my phone, I need to get the screen fixed. My buddy Dave had this beat-up '98 Civic in high school, and we used to spend hours trying to fix it up. We thought we were hot stuff because we put a spoiler on it and painted the brake calipers red. It looked ridiculous, honestly. Dave actually ended up selling that car to a guy who turned it into a demolition derby car. Can you imagine? A tiny little Civic in a demolition derby. I went to one of those derbies once at the county fair. The noise was insane, just metal crunching everywhere. They had these giant turkey legs at the fair, too. I don't know how they cook them, but they always taste like ham. Have you ever noticed that? Turkey legs at fairs just taste like salty ham. I tried to make one at home once, but it just came out dry. My mom told me I didn't brine it long enough, but who has time to let a piece of meat sit in salt water for two days? \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, problems with relationships and stuff. Yeah, it's frustrating. I'm just not moving forward with the mechanic stuff because I keep messing up the paperwork and deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Did your parents or teachers ever comment on your behavior or attention span when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. Even back in elementary school, my teachers were constantly moving my desk around. Like, they'd put me right next to the teacher's desk, or in the very back corner, because I was always talking out of turn or forgetting my homework. I'd rush through worksheets just to get them done, and I'd make all these stupid, careless mistakes. \n\nI remember Mrs. Gable in third grade—she had this giant jar of marbles on her desk. Every time the class was good, she'd drop a marble in, and if we filled it, we got a pizza party. But she'd always threaten to take marbles out if I didn't sit still. She was really strict, but she smelled like peppermint all the time. I actually love peppermint. Especially those little red and white swirly candies. My grandma always had a bowl of those in her living room, right next to this creepy porcelain doll she got at a flea market. The doll had these glass eyes that literally followed you around the room. I swear it was haunted. My cousin Joey tried to hide it in the closet once, and my grandma chased him out of the house with a wooden spoon. Joey was always getting into trouble, though. He once tried to build a ramp for his skateboard out of some old plywood and a milk crate, and he ended up breaking his collarbone. He had to wear this weird harness thing for like six weeks... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, teachers and stuff. Yeah, they were always on my case about rushing through work and talking too much.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities where you become so focused that you lose track of time and forget about everything else?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. I mean, I guess I can't really think of anything where I just sit there and get completely lost in it. I'm usually bouncing around too much. Like, even when I'm trying to play video games, I'll play for maybe ten minutes and then I have to get up and get a snack or check my phone. \n\nSpeaking of my phone, I need to get the screen fixed. I dropped it on the driveway yesterday when I was trying to carry in like, six bags of groceries at once. You know when you just refuse to make two trips? I had bags looped up to my elbows, and the one with the milk started slipping, so I tried to juggle it, and boom, phone right on the concrete. It's totally shattered. It's an older phone anyway, though, so maybe I should just get a new one. But they're so expensive now. My buddy Dave—the one with the Civic—he just got that new one with the three cameras on the back. It takes really good pictures, especially in the dark. We went to this bonfire last weekend out by the lake, and he got this awesome picture of the sparks flying up into the sky. It was a good fire, too. We used this old pallet wood we found behind a strip mall. It burned super hot. Some guy brought a guitar, but he only knew how to play \"Wonderwall,\" which was kind of annoying after the third time. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really get hyper-focused on stuff. I'm pretty much always all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless are more related to problems with organization and time management or with being easily distracted by your surroundings?\n\nPatient: I think it's mostly the organization part. Like, if I have a bunch of things to do—say I need to call the career counselor, clean up my room, and finally send in that application for the auto tech program—I just look at it all and I have no idea where to start. So I end up bouncing around between them and just half-finishing everything. \n\nLike yesterday, I started cleaning my room because I couldn't find my favorite hoodie. It's this gray one with a grease stain on the sleeve that I can't get out. I've tried everything—dish soap, baking soda, even this weird industrial cleaner my uncle gave me. My uncle works at a dry cleaner, actually. He’s always smelling like those weird chemicals. He used to bring home these huge rolls of plastic garment bags, and when we were kids, my cousins and I would use them as slip-and-slides in the backyard. We'd just put the hose on them and go flying. One time I slid right off the plastic and into my mom’s rose bushes. Man, those thorns tore up my leg pretty bad. I still have a little scar on my shin from it. It's shaped kind of like a lightning bolt, which is pretty cool, honestly. Reminds me of Harry Potter. I tried reading those books once, but I could never get past the second one. The movies were okay, though. \n\nAnyway... yeah, I just can't figure out how to prioritize things. I just jump from one task to another and nothing ever actually gets done.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself daydreaming or staring off into space when you're supposed to be focused on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. It's really frustrating, actually. Like, I'll be trying to figure something out—say I start watching a repair video on YouTube, or I'm trying to read the manual for a new tool I just bought. I'll be looking right at it, but after a few minutes, I realize I've just been staring at the screen or the page, and my mind is completely somewhere else. I have to back the video up or re-read the whole page because literally none of it stuck. \n\nI was actually trying to fix my toaster last week. It's one of those retro-looking ones, chrome, really shiny. I got it at a thrift store for like five bucks. Thrift stores are great, honestly. You can find some crazy stuff in there. I found this vintage leather jacket once, fit me perfectly, but it smelled like mothballs and old soup. I tried airing it out on the porch for like three days, but a stray cat ended up sleeping on it, so I just threw it away. That cat still hangs around my porch, actually. I call him Barnaby. He's missing half of his left ear, looks like he's been in some serious scraps. I bought a bag of cat food for him, the kind with the little fish shapes, but he only eats the red ones. I don't know why, maybe they taste different? I should probably look that up. \n\nBut yeah, the toaster. I was watching a video on how to take the heating element out, and I just totally zoned out. I ended up watching like three minutes of the guy talking before I realized I was thinking about what kind of sandwich I wanted to make. I had to rewind it like four times just to get through one step.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless have been present for as long as you can remember, or did they start at some point in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, as far back as I can remember, honestly. It's not like a new thing. Even back in elementary school, it was always the same story. My teachers were constantly moving my seat because I wouldn't stop talking out of turn, or I'd forget my homework, or I'd just blow through the worksheets and make a ton of careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done. \n\nI remember my third-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable. She moved my desk right next to hers, facing the chalkboard, so I couldn't look at anyone else. She had this weird smell, kind of like peppermint and old coffee. She always had a thermos of coffee on her desk, one of those old plaid Stanley ones. My grandpa had one just like it. He used to take it fishing with him. We'd go out to this little pond near his house, catch bluegill mostly. They're super bony, not really good for eating, but it was fun to catch them. We used live worms, and I remember feeling bad for them when we put them on the hook. My grandpa told me they didn't feel it, but I don't know if I believe that now. Worms are pretty complex, right? Like, if you cut them in half, they turn into two worms. Or is that a myth? I saw a documentary once about these deep-sea worms that live near hydrothermal vents. They look like giant tubes of lipstick. Nature is crazy like that. \n\nBut yeah, the school stuff. It's just always been like this. I've always just been kind of... all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with staying on track and feeling restless in most areas of your life, such as at home, at work, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much everywhere. It's not just a school thing or whatever. Like, at home, I can't even get through a simple chore without getting sidetracked, and hanging out with friends is just as bad. I'll be in the middle of a conversation and suddenly I'm just... not there. I get fidgety, or I'll interrupt people because if I don't say what's in my head right then, I'll completely forget it. \n\nI did that to my buddy Mark the other day. We were at this diner downtown, the one with the really good milkshakes. They serve them in those big metal cups, you know? So you get the glass and then the extra in the tin. I always get strawberry. It's weird because I hate actual strawberries—the texture of the seeds just bothers me—but artificial strawberry flavor is the best. It's like banana flavor. Did you know the artificial banana flavor is based on a type of banana that went extinct in the 1950s? The Gros Michel, I think it was called. Now we eat Cavendish bananas, but the candy still tastes like the old ones. It's wild that we're basically tasting a ghost fruit. I read this whole article about a fungus that wiped them out, and now the same fungus is threatening the bananas we have now. We might not even have bananas in like, twenty years. That would be crazy. I wonder what they'd replace them with in smoothies. Mangoes, maybe? Mangoes are pretty good, but they're a pain to cut. You always lose half the fruit trying to get around that massive pit in the middle. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, Mark. Yeah, I kept cutting him off while he was talking about his car troubles because I remembered I needed to buy wiper fluid, and I just blurted it out. He got pretty annoyed. So yeah, it definitely happens everywhere.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, it's definitely both, but the detail stuff is what really gets me in trouble lately. Like, I've been trying to get into this automotive training program, right? And when I'm filling out the forms or looking over these parts lists they give you to study, I'll just miss one little box, or I'll copy a serial number wrong. He's got this old Honda Civic, like a 2002 or something. Those things run forever if you treat them right. That guy had like, twenty cars on his property, just rusting away. It was like a graveyard. I always wonder what happens to all those old cars. Do they just sit there forever? I guess eventually they just turn into dust, right? Everything turns into dust eventually. I saw this video about how the sun is going to expand and swallow the Earth in like, five billion years. So none of it really matters anyway, I guess. 

But yeah, I'll just miss a detail on those forms, and it's so frustrating because I know the answer, my brain just skips right over it. And then I'm also bouncing my leg the whole time I'm trying to fill it out, so it's just a mess all around.

You always lose half the fruit trying to get around that massive pit in the middle. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, Mark. My uncle had a Civic that went for like 300,000 miles before the transmission finally gave out. He ended up selling it for parts to this guy who lived out in the woods. That guy had like, twenty cars on his property, just rusting away. It was like a graveyard. I always wonder what happens to all those old cars. Do they just sit there forever? I guess eventually they just turn into dust, right? Everything turns into dust eventually. I saw this video about how the sun is going to expand and swallow the Earth in like, five billion years.
